# S14a part 2 record measurements

Generated by `research/pde_ledger_v3/scripts/S14a_2_record_measurements.py` from starting commit `978c359c`.

Commands run from the repository root. Each Mnn block is the literal stdout of
`python3 research/pde_ledger_v3/scripts/S14a_2_record_measurements.py --section Mnn`; inner commands and their literal stdout are included.
Named-block retrievals and census counts are text retrieval and exact literal matches only.
The filed witness scripts are read as text, never imported or executed. Their stdout is
saved review evidence, not a new run or an accepted part 2 build. The checksum manifest
and historical rerun reports are retrieved as filed; no new verification run is claimed.

## M01 — Record contract, its gate and commit provenance

````````text
$ python3 research/pde_ledger_v3/scripts/S14a_2_record_measurements.py --section M01
$ cat research/pde_ledger_v3/directives/S14a_2_record_decision_list.md
# S14a part 2 record: the slow limit and far flux, recorded as a requirement (pre-builder decision list)

**Author:** Claude (orchestrator), 2026-10-21. **Author of the record:** Codex. `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` apply.
Paths are relative to `research/pde_ledger_v3/`. The lookups behind this list are in
`directives/_measurements/S14a_2_record_setup_lookups.md` ("L-n") and in the review lookups
`directives/_measurements/S14a_2_record_decision_list_review_p1_lookups.md` ("N-n").

**Gate.** This is a pre-builder decision list. It gets one Codex + Grok pass and is folded once (G2). The record
author starts only after that. The record is physics-bearing prose. Two non-author legs (fresh Claude + Grok) review
it, source-first, until clear on what may be claimed. This follows the S14a part 1 record
(`directives/S14a_1_record_decision_list.md`). The G2 pass is complete and this revision is its one fold
(`directives/_measurements/S14a_2_record_decision_list_review_p1_disposition.md`; reviewed text at `ee6791c1`).

## User decisions this list records
- **Framing** (2026-10-19) and **"Keep S12"** (2026-10-20), as the step's decision list carries them.
- **This part covers both remaining objects** (2026-10-21), and the two decisions **"Steady throughput"** and
  **"Exchange live"** (2026-10-21), as the step's decision list quotes them.
- **"Record as requirement"** (2026-10-21), after the step's decision list's second review pass. The user selected
  this option; the wording is the orchestrator's option text: "No build. Record that the slab's own dynamics (the
  open S12 forms) must pick the slow limit and the far behaviour, as a register requirement on the brane.
  S14/S15/S20 stay CONDITIONAL, now on that named requirement."

## What must be true
1. **What was attempted, and its standing.** The record states what the step's decision list named, in each
   revision, and what each review pass found and how it was disposed of. No build ran, by the user's decision. The
   record presents no computed object of S14a part 2 and no result of a build.
2. **The evidence, as filed.** Every statement about why the slow limit or the far behaviour was not determined rests
   on the review witnesses and the dispositions, as filed. Each statement cites its retrieval command, and the
   commands with their literal output go in the measurements file.
   - Only retrieval is allowed: existence, verbatim retrieval, literal-match counts, and the shape of a named stored
     object.
   - Each witness is stated with its pass, its leg and its standing: a review leg's instrument, not an accepted
     build.
   - Each witness is described by what its own source and its disposition say it constructs, checks and compares,
     and by no more (N4). Neither a general theorem nor the orchestrator's reading in the pass-2 disposition is
     presented as a computed result. That reading is quoted as a reading, with its source.
3. **The plan's objects.** The record states which of the plan's four S14a objects (`V3_STEP_PLAN.md:636–637`) are
   printed, and by which part, as its sources state them. It states that the remaining two are not derived, and the
   standing of S14, S15 and S20 as the plan gives it (`:639`). Nothing is claimed for them beyond what the user's
   decision states.
4. **What is handed on.** Under the register's own schema, rest-on test and method, both routes included, the pass
   hands on what the user's decision names: that the slab's own dynamics select the slow limit and the far
   behaviour.
   - The requirement names the object required, not a derivation route, law, material, premise or candidate.
   - It cites the record's statements and the filed evidence it rests on, and its consumers: S14, S15 and S20.
   - Its target follows the sources' owners (the S12 record for the forms, under "Keep S12").
   - It merges into an existing entry where one covers the same requirement, rather than duplicating it.
   - Where this list and the register disagree, the register wins, and the disagreement is reported.
5. **What remains open.** Each item the step's decision list retains as OPEN is stated with its owner, as the sources
   state them. The readings the user did not choose are listed as not computed. None is closed here.
6. **Status and provenance lines.**
   - S14a part 1's record and register pass were accepted at `4a4dfaf5` (L4). In the part 1 record, the register (its
     status and its S14a part 1 pass) and `../../STATUS.md`, every statement that presents them as not yet accepted,
     or their review as still to come, states that acceptance instead, heading or not. The lines found so far are in
     L7 and N5; that list is not claimed to be complete. Statements about S12's earlier state stay as they are.
   - The two notes carried from the part 1 record's round-1 review are repaired in the part 1 record: the l.59
     citation names its actual source, and the §6 quotation of the earlier population directive is retrieved from
     that directive, `directives/S11_requirements_pass2_directive.md` (L7).
   - Nothing else in the part 1 record or the register's S14a part 1 pass changes.
   - `../../STATUS.md` states where S14a part 2 stands, as the record does.
7. **The account of edits.** The record names every file this work changes outside the record, its measurements
   file and its generator, with each change. That account matches `git diff` against the commit the work starts
   from, and the measurements file retrieves that diff.

## Exclusions
- No new CAS, derivation, witness, closure or profile choice. No rerun of any witness.
- Edit only:
  - the record, its measurements file and generator;
  - `SUBSTRATE_REQUIREMENTS.md`;
  - `steps/S14a_1_projection.md`, for item 6 only;
  - `../../STATUS.md`.
- No commit and no push.

## STOP report (at most one page)
- The record's headline claims, each with its source.
- The evidence each claim rests on, with its pass, leg and standing.
- Register entry counts before and after, new entries by target, and existing entries that gained a source.
- Disagreements between sources, with both sides quoted.
- Open questions that need computation beyond retrieval.
- The account of edits (item 7).

## Entry schema

| field | meaning |
|---|---|
| `id` | stable identifier, cited from both ends |
| `source` | the step that needs it |
| `target` | the step that must deliver it |
| `requirement` | **the object required**, named — not a derivation path for it |
| `on failure` | what breaks in the source step if the target cannot deliver |
| `status` | OPEN · DELIVERED · RETIRED (with the commit that changed it) |

Name the object; do not prescribe how the target should obtain it. A requirement that specifies a
derivation route manufactures arguments about the route — see `CLAUDE.md` rule 3.

Distinguish carefully:
- a **requirement** is an obligation on a *future* step;
- a **postulate** is a value or form assumed *here* and not derived — that belongs in `ANSATZ_LEDGER.md`;
- a **defect** is something already wrong — that belongs in `DEFECT_REGISTER.md`.

A postulate with a named retirement condition generates a requirement. `B_comp` below is exactly that.

---

# S14a part 2 record decision list, review pass 1: dispositions (orchestrator)

The mechanical lookups behind each disposition are in `S14a_2_record_decision_list_review_p1_lookups.md`, beside
this file (generator `_scratch/s14a/gen/s14a2_recdl_p1_lookups.sh`). "N-n" cites a section of that file. Every
computed result quoted here is a leg's saved stdout.

## The pass

- **Artifact.** `directives/S14a_2_record_decision_list.md`, orchestrator-written, sha256 `cf7a5445…`, with
  `S14a_2_record_setup_lookups.md` (`4a4e433e…`) and the filed witnesses `S14a_2_review_witnesses/` with their
  checksum file `S14a_2_review_witnesses.sha256` (`f090dde1…`). All three are preserved unchanged in the commit that
  adds this file. All matched the baseline after the pass, and the working tree was unchanged apart from the lookups
  file itself (N1).
- **Role.** A pre-builder decision list for a physics-bearing record (G2: one Codex + Grok pass, fold once). The
  record it governs is reviewed until clear by fresh Claude + Grok after Codex writes it.
- **Prompt and legs.** `_scratch/s14a2/recdl_review_prompt_p1.md`, sha256 `38bcd63f…` (N1). Both legs received it
  identically, in parallel. Codex (xhigh, workspace-write in its scratch) wrote
  `/var/projects/_review_scratch/s14a2-recdl-p1-codex/REVIEW.md`, and Grok (`grok-4.7`, effort high) wrote
  `/var/projects/_review_scratch/s14a2-recdl-p1-grok/REVIEW.md`. Both exited 0 (N1).
- **Verdicts.** Codex: FINDINGS, 1. Grok: FINDINGS, 2 (N2).
- **What both legs reproduced.** Each reran the seven filed witness scripts. All seven stdout files matched the
  filed ones, with empty stderr (N4).
- **What Grok checked and found usable as written:** the quoted user option; the plan's four S14a objects and the
  CONDITIONAL standing of S14, S15 and S20; the register's schema, rest-on test and both routes; that `R-S12-02`
  does not already state the new requirement; and the two carried citation notes.

## Findings

**S1. Item 2 gives every witness one common reach that one witness does not have.** (Codex 1, Grok 2.)
**CONFIRMED.**
- **The list.** "A statement reaches only as far as the witnesses reach: particular solutions of the committed
  balances on which one choice of family, average or order of limits gives one value and another choice another"
  (l.56–57, N3).
- **The source.** The pass-2 Grok witness's first section states its own scope: "Spherical projected balance, fixed
  faces, no face channel", with `Σ = c t` and `F = −c r/3 + β t/r²` checked against that one balance (N4). The
  pass-2 disposition's R3 uses it for "two readings" of the volume-flux gate (N4). That is neither a choice of
  family, average or order of limits, nor a checked solution of both committed balances. The other witnesses
  describe themselves in other terms ("A fixed admissible field configuration…", "Small admissible shell witness,
  not a construction of the proposed build", "Two exact solution probes, not an S14a construction or acceptance
  test", and so on; N4).
- **Effect.** The record could state, for the pass-2 Grok witness, a stronger verification than that witness
  carries.
- **Cause.** The list wrote one class from the orchestrator's reading of the witnesses, rather than pointing at
  what each witness and its disposition state.

**S2. Item 6's census misses three lines that still present part 1 as unaccepted, and its last bullet then
freezes two of them.** (Grok 1.) **CONFIRMED.**
- **The list.** "Every line that still describes them as written for, under or awaiting review states that
  acceptance instead, heading or not … (L7)", then "Nothing else in the part 1 record or the register's S14a part 1
  pass changes" (l.75–81, N3).
- **The sources.** L7's predicate prints four lines: record l.3, register l.8 and l.1483, `STATUS.md` l.11 (N5). A
  wider literal search of the same files also prints:
  - `STATUS.md:1`, "S14a part 1 record written for review";
  - `SUBSTRATE_REQUIREMENTS.md:1486`, "This record/pass is written, not yet accepted.";
  - `steps/S14a_1_projection.md:455`, "source-first record review remains next under the contract." (N5).
- **Effect.** The register's S14a part 1 pass would state acceptance in its heading and "not yet accepted" in its
  next sentence. The part 1 record would state acceptance at l.3 and pending review at l.455. Two authors could
  read the frozen lines differently.
- **Cause.** The list fixed the census by a phrase, not by the property the lines must meet.
- **Not affected.** Record l.359 and l.444 describe S12's earlier state and its repair. They are statements about
  S12, not about part 1 (N5).

## Agreement between the legs

The legs agree on S1, found independently. Only Grok found S2. The census in N5 bears it out.

## Fold

Both findings change what the record may claim or what it changes. G2 folds them once, in a separate commit after
this pass is preserved:
- **S1:** item 2 states that each witness is described by what its own source and its disposition say it constructs
  and checks, and by no more.
- **S2:** item 6 names the property: in the three files, every statement that presents part 1's record or register
  pass as not yet accepted, or its review as still to come, states the acceptance. The lookups list the lines found
  (N5) without claiming that the list is complete. Statements about S12's earlier state stay as they are.

```
$ sed -n '27,36p' /var/projects/toy_physics/research/pde_ledger_v3/directives/_measurements/S14a_2_review_witnesses/p2-grok_dc_limit_objects.py
print("=== 1. Storage of a density that changes without bound ===")
# Spherical projected balance, fixed faces, no face channel:
#   d/dt Sigma + (1/r^2) d/dr (r^2 F) = 0
#   Sigma = c*t,  F = -c*r/3 + beta*t/r**2
Sigma = c * t
F = -c * R / 3 + beta * t / R**2
div_term = sp.simplify(sp.diff(R**2 * F, R) / R**2)
show("partial_t Sigma", sp.diff(Sigma, t))
show("radial divergence of F", div_term)
show("balance residual", sp.simplify(sp.diff(Sigma, t) + div_term))
```

$ sed -n '62,71p' /var/projects/toy_physics/research/pde_ledger_v3/directives/_measurements/S14a_2_decision_list_review_p2_disposition.md
**R3. "Nonzero over the average" is not one domain for the volume flux.** (Grok 1.) **CONFIRMED.**
- **The list.** The volume flux is printed "where the projected material density at the outer radius is nonzero
  over the average" (l.133–135, M3). Part 1's condition was `Σ(R,t) ≠ 0` (part 1 decision list l.93–98, M8).
- **The evidence.** Grok's solution has `Σ = c t`, nonzero for every `t > 0`, with no finite average. The average of
  the whole volume-flux quotient exists and equals `4*pi*beta/c`. Reading the gate as "the average of `Σ` is finite
  and nonzero" prints nothing; reading it as "`Σ ≠ 0` over the window and the quotient's average exists" prints
  `4*pi*beta/c`. On this solution the separate averages of the outer flux and the core channel diverge, while
  their balance with storage averages to `0` (M6).
- **Effect.** Item 3 prints a different object under each reading, and one of them omits the throughput on a
  steadily spreading solution.
```
````````

## M02 — Part 2 decision list, revisions 1 and 2

````````text
$ python3 research/pde_ledger_v3/scripts/S14a_2_record_measurements.py --section M02
$ git show d72bb551:research/pde_ledger_v3/directives/S14a_2_decision_list.md
# S14a part 2: steady throughput, the return and the far flux (pre-builder decision list)

**Author:** Claude (orchestrator), 2026-10-21. **Author of the build:** Codex. `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` apply.
Paths are relative to `research/pde_ledger_v3/`. The setup lookups behind each pointer are in
`directives/_measurements/S14a_2_setup_lookups.md`.

**Gate.** This is a pre-builder decision list. It gets one Codex + Grok pass and is folded once (G2). It also names the
objects the build computes, so that content is reviewed until clear (spec row). The builder starts only after that.

## Filing and framing
- **The plan's step.** S14a, "The drain bridge" (`V3_STEP_PLAN.md:626`), asks for "the projected order-loss source ·
  the definition of `J` · the profile-dependent `J → Q` map · the controlled `ω → 0` return law" (`:636–637`).
  Until it succeeds, "S14, S15 and S20 are CONDITIONAL, not carried results" (`:639`).
- **Part 1 is accepted** (build `94fddf2f`, comparator `de81b9fc`, record `4a4dfaf5`). It prints the first two
  objects, with flux objects at a finite outer radius (`steps/S14a_1_projection.md:143–150`). It records "No
  steady/no-leakage, constant-density, radius-independent flux or far-radius limit is earned here" (`:140–141`).
  Its OPEN table leaves the return law to "A later part of S14a" and the `J → Q` map with no named later part
  (`:376–377`).
- **User decision, 2026-10-21: this part covers both remaining objects**, the `ω → 0` return law and the `J → Q` map.
- **The slow limit is derived, never posed.** The plan: "If a static limit is ever wanted it must be **derived** as
  the `ω→0` limit of the dynamical law, ⛔ never posed as an independent frozen solve" (`V3_STEP_PLAN.md:599–601`).
  The S12 decision list: "Any slow or static limit is derived from this law, never posed"
  (`directives/S12_decision_list.md:48–49`).
- **The framing carries over** (user, 2026-10-19). This part states what the committed law gives and picks no drain
  law and no return law's form. Each force states the flow it needs, and S21 compares them. Under "Keep S12" (user,
  2026-10-20), the conversion law's forms stay S12's and stay OPEN.

## Supplied equations (committed; unfalsifiable in this build)
The committed law, user decision of 2026-07-24 (`V3_STEP_PLAN.md:589–601`;
`../pde_ledger_v2/notes/stages/ledger_stage006_two_phase_chiB_ontology.md:78–84`):

```
∂_t n + ∇₄·(n u) = 0                                   (total material)
∂_t(χ_B n) + ∇₄·(χ_B n u + J_χ) = n Γ_B ,   Γ_B = Γ_return − Γ_drain ,   [Γ_B] = T⁻¹
```
- **The fields** are part 1's, on the same 4D domain: in-plane `x^i` (three coordinates), bulk direction `w`, and
  time `t`. In this part `Γ_return` and `Γ_drain` are two separate live functions, as the committed law writes them;
  `Γ_B` is their difference. Stage 006's sign: "`Gamma_B < 0` = brane→bulk de-structuring (drain); `Gamma_B > 0` =
  bulk→brane re-ordering (return)" (`:86–87`). No sign is imposed on `Γ_B` or on either part.
- **U1 and its encoding** are as the S12 decision list states them (revision 4, cleared at `b08673a2`): "How U1
  enters", and item 1's "Fields at a face". The shear-holding region is bounded by two live faces
  `w₋(x,t) < w₊(x,t)`, and `χ_B` vanishes outside it. Both are adopted premises, not results of this build.
- **The material factor at a face**, as the accepted S12 record writes it (`steps/S12_order_source.md:79–80`), for
  region `r` at face `s`, using that region's one-sided trace at `w = w_s`:

  ```
  a_{r,s} = u_r^w − ∂_t w_s − u_r^i ∂_i w_s
  ```

  It is supplied and unfalsifiable in this build. The build's face terms are reached by its own integration.
- **Part 1's objects** are the accepted definitions this part takes the limit of: the projected balances (part 1
  item 1) and, in the spherical specialization, the face channel, the core channel, `J`, the order lost, storage and
  the outer flux (part 1 items 2–3; notation `steps/S14a_1_projection.md:80–127`). The SymPy engine may reuse the
  accepted part 1 SymPy construction (`scripts/S14a_1_projection.py`, accepted at `94fddf2f`). The `.wl` imports
  nothing and re-derives.

## User decisions on the limit and the far region (2026-10-21)
The user selected these two options. The wording is the orchestrator's option text.
- **The slow limit: "Steady throughput."** "The throat is at rest, and the drain and re-ordering run at constant
  rates. The pattern stops changing, except that the slab's steady spreading stays a live term. The flow runs
  throughout. No re-ordering form is picked; the forms stay S12's."
- **The far region: "Exchange live."** "Print how the far flux depends on radius, keeping the exchange and storage
  between the throat and the sphere. Take a far-radius limit only where the build shows one exists. Any condition it
  needs is printed, not imposed."

Not chosen: the slowly moving throat, with drive frequency `ω = k·V` (the G0 card's reading,
`../../software/em_charge_attribute/g0_closure_card_v0.md:396`); v2's exchange-free far region as a specialization;
and a split of the far flux into a throat share and a re-ordering share.

**How steady throughput enters (the user's definition, made operational here).**
- **Constant rates.** The drain and re-ordering source densities, `n Γ_drain` and `n Γ_return`, are independent of
  time. Each stays a live function of position.
- **The spreading stays live.** Neither the faces' motion nor the change in time of the projected material is set to
  zero. Whatever part of either persists in the limit stays a live object. The slab may spread by thickening, by
  growing in area, or both; neither is chosen.
- **What else it requires is derived.** What steady throughput requires of `n`, `u`, `χ_B`, `J_χ` and the faces is
  derived by the build from the time-dependent balances, and printed. It is not supplied here.

## What the build computes
Both engines construct these independently, with the `.wl` blind.

1. **The steady-throughput limit of the projected balances.** Start from the time-dependent projected balances of
   part 1 item 1, and take the limit as defined above.
   - Print each requirement the limit places on the fields and faces, as an object.
   - Print both projected balances in the limit. The return and drain parts of the projected order source, and its
     face parts, are printed separately.
   - **What the limit drops.** For each balance, print the time-dependent object, the limit object and their
     difference, so nothing is dropped unseen.
   - Every field not constrained by a printed requirement stays live.
2. **The return in steady throughput.** In part 1's labelled specialization (spherical symmetry about the throat, the
   shell between an inner sphere around the excluded core and an outer sphere) and in the limit of item 1, print
   separately:
   - **the re-ordering total** over the shell, from `Γ_return`, and **the drain total**, from `Γ_drain`;
   - **the order lost** over the shell, as part 1 defines it;
   - **the order budget over the shell:** the storage of projected order, the in-plane order flux through each
     sphere, the order crossing the faces, and the projected order source, with its return, drain and face parts
     separate;
   - **the material budget over the shell:** part 1's face channel, core channel, `J`, storage and outer flux, and
     the balance relating them.

   It also prints the balance that relates the re-ordering total to the drain total and the other shell objects.
3. **The far flux with exchange live** (user decision above). In the same specialization and limit, print as
   functions of the outer radius:
   - every object of item 2;
   - the outer material flux and, where the projected material density at the outer radius is nonzero, the volume
     flux of the projected velocity through the outer sphere. That condition is carried wherever the velocity or its
     volume flux is printed;
   - the dependence of each on the outer radius, with the exchange and storage between the inner and outer spheres
     kept.

   A far-radius limit is printed only where the build shows it exists. The condition on the live fields that it needs
   is printed as an object, never imposed.
4. **Dimensional homogeneity** of every new object in items 1–3.

A name may name an object, never its value or a comparison outcome. The script prints objects and never states
conclusions. No acceptance criterion references an expected value. Spherical symmetry is labelled as a
specialization wherever it is printed. Steady throughput is labelled as the user's limit wherever it is printed.
Neither is a limit earned from the general balances.

## Model point
- **Regime:** steady throughput, the user's limit (2026-10-21), reached from the time-dependent balances. The
  time-dependent objects are printed beside it.
- **Freezes:** none intended beyond the user's definition. `J_χ` stays live, the faces keep their motion unless a
  printed requirement constrains it, and no exchange sign is imposed: U2 leaves the sign free. U3 and U4 enter no
  computed object, as in S12 (`steps/S12_order_source.md:154–155`). The user's premise that brane material leaves only
  in throats (2026-10-06) is not imposed.
- **Transfer:** wherever the committed law holds and the shear-holding region has two faces over the in-plane
  coordinates. The puncture's core is excluded and stays with S22.

## Inventories (for the record, under its own review; not computed, not used by the builder)
The record points at each source row, without restating it:
- **v2's `J → Q` map:**
  - `../../software/stage1_solver/reports/pathA_21_emergent_G_mass_bridge.md:19`, `:22` and `:62–66`: the rows for
    `S_leak`, `W_eff`, `N_∞,3`, `J` and `Θ_Q`;
  - `../pde_ledger_v2/notes/stages/ledger_stage004_gnls_action_dimensional_foundation.md:148–150`.
  The record compares what this build prints with these, by retrieval. Where the comparison needs computation, it is
  listed as open.
- **The picture the plan's item was written against:** the plan's text at `bcbc2716` (2026-07-31); the G0 card's
  return kernel, its stationary limit and its order of limits
  (`../../software/em_charge_attribute/g0_closure_card_v0.md:291–322` and `:387–398`). The G0 drain is ruled out
  (`V3_STEP_PLAN.md:597–601`); the card is a comparison only.
- **The steps that wait on S14a:** S14 (`V3_STEP_PLAN.md:643–646`), and S15's source-map row (`:663–665`). The record
  states which of S14a's four objects are now printed, and the standing of S14, S15 and S20 as the plan gives it.
- **Part 1's carried notes:** the stale status lines and the two notes in
  `directives/_measurements/S14a_1_record_r1_review_disposition.md:35–50`.

## Retained OPEN
- the link between `J` and mass (C3, a gap), and any link to orbital `GM`;
- the core's interior (S22);
- the flow profile (each force's requirement; S21);
- the slab's laws and S12's forms, with the owners the accepted S12 record gives (`steps/S12_order_source.md:292`).
  These include the forms of `Γ_return`, `Γ_drain` and `J_χ`, and the return controllers;
- the material branch, A13 (S1's gate, propagated through S5/S12);
- the readings the user did not choose (above). They are not computed, and no owner is named.

## Intended claim
What the committed law gives in the user's steady-throughput limit, for the projected balances. For a throat at rest
with spherical symmetry: the re-ordering and drain totals over a shell, the order and material budgets, `J`'s two
channels and the outer flux. How each depends on the outer radius, with exchange live. What the limit requires of the
fields, and what it drops. A far-radius limit only where one is printed with its condition. It is not a mass, a drain
law, a return form, a throat solve or a flow.

## Next
A build directive for Codex derived from this list. Then:
- G1 review of the build (fresh Claude + Grok), until clear;
- a cross-engine comparison;
- the record and its register pass, each reviewed until clear.

# S14a part 2: steady throughput, the return and the far flux (pre-builder decision list)

**Author:** Claude (orchestrator), 2026-10-21. **Author of the build:** Codex. `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` apply.

**Revision 2.** Revision 2 is the one fold of the G2 pass (dispositions:

defines the limit (Q1, Q2) and names the totals and budgets exactly (Q3).

## Filing and framing
- **The plan's step.** S14a, "The drain bridge" (`V3_STEP_PLAN.md:626`), asks for "the projected order-loss source ·

  Its OPEN table leaves the return law to "A later part of S14a" and the `J → Q` map with no named later part
  (`:376–377`).
- **User decision, 2026-10-21: this part covers both remaining objects**, the `ω → 0` return law and the `J → Q` map.
- **The slow limit is derived, never posed.** The plan: "If a static limit is ever wanted it must be **derived** as
  the `ω→0` limit of the dynamical law, ⛔ never posed as an independent frozen solve" (`V3_STEP_PLAN.md:599–601`).
  The S12 decision list: "Any slow or static limit is derived from this law, never posed"
  (`directives/S12_decision_list.md:48–49`).
- **The framing carries over** (user, 2026-10-19). This part states what the committed law gives and picks no drain
  law and no return law's form. Each force states the flow it needs, and S21 compares them. Under "Keep S12" (user,
  2026-10-20), the conversion law's forms stay S12's and stay OPEN.

## Supplied equations (committed; unfalsifiable in this build)
The committed law, user decision of 2026-07-24 (`V3_STEP_PLAN.md:589–601`;
`../pde_ledger_v2/notes/stages/ledger_stage006_two_phase_chiB_ontology.md:78–84`):

```
∂_t n + ∇₄·(n u) = 0                                   (total material)
∂_t(χ_B n) + ∇₄·(χ_B n u + J_χ) = n Γ_B ,   Γ_B = Γ_return − Γ_drain ,   [Γ_B] = T⁻¹
```
- **The fields** are part 1's, on the same 4D domain: in-plane `x^i` (three coordinates), bulk direction `w`, and
  time `t`. In this part `Γ_return` and `Γ_drain` are two separate live functions, as the committed law writes them;
  `Γ_B` is their difference. Stage 006's sign: "`Gamma_B < 0` = brane→bulk de-structuring (drain); `Gamma_B > 0` =
  bulk→brane re-ordering (return)" (`:86–87`). No sign is imposed on `Γ_B` or on either part.
- **U1 and its encoding** are as the S12 decision list states them (revision 4, cleared at `b08673a2`): "How U1
  enters", and item 1's "Fields at a face". The shear-holding region is bounded by two live faces
  `w₋(x,t) < w₊(x,t)`, and `χ_B` vanishes outside it. Both are adopted premises, not results of this build.
- **The material factor at a face**, as the accepted S12 record writes it (`steps/S12_order_source.md:79–80`), for
  region `r` at face `s`, using that region's one-sided trace at `w = w_s`:

  ```
  a_{r,s} = u_r^w − ∂_t w_s − u_r^i ∂_i w_s
  ```

  It is supplied and unfalsifiable in this build. The build's face terms are reached by its own integration.
- **Part 1's objects** are the accepted definitions this part takes the limit of: the projected balances (part 1
  item 1) and, in the spherical specialization, the face channel, the core channel, `J`, the order lost, storage and
  the outer flux (part 1 items 2–3; notation `steps/S14a_1_projection.md:80–127`). The SymPy engine may reuse the
  accepted part 1 SymPy construction (`scripts/S14a_1_projection.py`, accepted at `94fddf2f`). The `.wl` imports
  nothing and re-derives.

## User decisions on the limit and the far region (2026-10-21)
The user selected these two options. The wording is the orchestrator's option text.
- **The slow limit: "Steady throughput."** "The throat is at rest, and the drain and re-ordering run at constant
  rates. The pattern stops changing, except that the slab's steady spreading stays a live term. The flow runs
  throughout. No re-ordering form is picked; the forms stay S12's."
- **The far region: "Exchange live."** "Print how the far flux depends on radius, keeping the exchange and storage
  between the throat and the sphere. Take a far-radius limit only where the build shows one exists. Any condition it
  needs is printed, not imposed."

Not chosen: the slowly moving throat, with drive frequency `ω = k·V` (the G0 card's reading,
`../../software/em_charge_attribute/g0_closure_card_v0.md:396`); v2's exchange-free far region as a specialization;
and a split of the far flux into a throat share and a re-ordering share.

**The limit: the DC part (`ω → 0`).** This defines the limit for every item below. The plan calls the frozen
completions it replaces "DC sink/return completions" (`V3_STEP_PLAN.md:633`). No condition is placed on
`n Γ_return`, `n Γ_drain` or any other native field at a point.
- **What it acts on.** The projected objects of part 1 item 1, which are functions of in-plane position and time:
  the time rates of the projected material and order densities, the projected fluxes, the face terms, and the
  projected order source and its parts (below). In the specialization, also the shell objects of items 2 and 3, at
  fixed inner and outer radii. The integration in `w` between the live faces comes first, so no frame in `w` is
  chosen. The in-plane frame is the throat's: the throat is at rest, at the origin of the specialization.
- **The operation.** The DC part of such an object `X` is its zero-frequency part, the long-time average

  ```
  ⟨X⟩ = lim_{T→∞} (1/T) ∫_{t₀}^{t₀+T} X dt
  ```

  at fixed in-plane position, or at fixed radii. Whether it exists is printed as a condition on the live fields,
  never imposed. The DC part of a product or a quotient is the DC part of that whole object.
- **What the limit drops** is `X − ⟨X⟩`, for each object.
- **Steady throughput, as the user chose it.** The DC parts do not change in time, so the drain and re-ordering run
  at constant rates. The DC part of each projected density's time rate is the slab's steady spreading, and it stays
  live. No DC part is set to zero, and the slab may spread by thickening, by growing in area, or both.
- **Order of limits.** The average is taken at fixed position and fixed radii. Any far-radius limit (item 3) is
  taken after it, on the DC objects.

**The parts of the projected order source.** Part 1's projected order source is `Sχ = ∫n₂Γ_B,2 dw + C₋ + C₊`
(`steps/S14a_1_projection.md:89`), where `C₋` and `C₊` are its parts concentrated on the faces (`:87`). With
`Γ_B = Γ_return − Γ_drain`:
- the **return part** is `∫n₂Γ_return,2 dw` and the **drain part** is `∫n₂Γ_drain,2 dw`, each over the regular part
  inside the region only;
- the **face part** is `C₋ + C₊`, net. It is not split into return and drain: no such split is supplied.

Each concentrated part is counted once, in the face part.

## What the build computes
Both engines construct these independently, with the `.wl` blind.

1. **The DC part of the projected balances.** Apply the operation above to both time-dependent projected balances
   of part 1 item 1.
   - Print the DC part of each balance. The DC parts of the return, drain and face parts of the projected order
     source are printed separately.
   - **What the limit drops.** For each balance, print the time-dependent object, its DC part and what the limit
     drops, so nothing is dropped unseen.
   - Every field stays live.
2. **The return at DC.** In part 1's labelled specialization (spherical symmetry about the throat, the shell between
   an inner sphere around the excluded core and an outer sphere), print the DC parts of, separately:
   - **the re-ordering total, the drain total and the face total:** the return, drain and face parts integrated over
     the shell, `∫shell` as part 1 writes it (`steps/S14a_1_projection.md:112`);
   - **the order lost** over the shell, as part 1 defines it (`Lχ = −∫shell Sχ`, `:125`);
   - **the order budget over the shell:** part 1's projected order balance (`:91`) integrated over the shell. Its
     terms are the storage of projected order, `∂t∫shell Σχ`; the in-plane order flux `4πr²H_r` (`H` as at `:84`),
     outward through the outer sphere and inward through the inner sphere, as part 1 orients its outer flux and core
     channel (`:126`, `:123`); the face terms as that balance carries them, after the full concentrated part
     (`:88`); and `∫shell Sχ`, as the three totals;
   - **the material budget over the shell:** part 1's face channel, core channel, `J`, storage and outer flux, and
     the balance relating them.

   For each budget, print the time-dependent object, its DC part and what the limit drops.
3. **The far flux with exchange live** (user decision above). In the same specialization, print as functions of the
   outer radius:
   - the DC part of every object of item 2;
   - the DC part of the outer material flux, and, where the projected material density at the outer radius is
     nonzero over the average, the DC part of the volume flux of the projected velocity through the outer sphere.
     That condition is carried wherever the velocity or its volume flux is printed;
   - the dependence of each on the outer radius, with the exchange and storage between the inner and outer spheres
     kept.

   A far-radius limit is printed only where the build shows it exists, after the average. The condition on the live
   fields that it needs is printed as an object, never imposed.
4. **Dimensional homogeneity** of every new object in items 1–3.

A name may name an object, never its value or a comparison outcome. The script prints objects and never states
conclusions. No acceptance criterion references an expected value. Spherical symmetry is labelled as a
specialization wherever it is printed; it is not a limit earned from item 1. The DC part is labelled as the user's
steady-throughput limit wherever it is printed.

## Model point
- **Regime:** the DC part (`ω → 0`), the user's steady throughput (2026-10-21), reached from the time-dependent
  balances by the operation above. The time-dependent objects and what the limit drops are printed beside it.
- **Freezes:** none intended. The operation acts on whole projected objects and sets no DC part to zero. `J_χ`
  stays live, the faces keep their motion, and no exchange sign is imposed: U2 leaves the sign free. U3 and U4 enter no
  computed object, as in S12 (`steps/S12_order_source.md:154–155`). The user's premise that brane material leaves only
  in throats (2026-10-06) is not imposed.
- **Transfer:** wherever the committed law holds and the shear-holding region has two faces over the in-plane
  coordinates. The puncture's core is excluded and stays with S22.

## Inventories (for the record, under its own review; not computed, not used by the builder)
The record points at each source row, without restating it:
- **v2's `J → Q` map:**
  - `../../software/stage1_solver/reports/pathA_21_emergent_G_mass_bridge.md:19`, `:22` and `:62–66`: the rows for
    `S_leak`, `W_eff`, `N_∞,3`, `J` and `Θ_Q`;
  - `../pde_ledger_v2/notes/stages/ledger_stage004_gnls_action_dimensional_foundation.md:148–150`.
  The record compares what this build prints with these, by retrieval. Where the comparison needs computation, it is
  listed as open.
- **The picture the plan's item was written against:** the plan's text at `bcbc2716` (2026-07-31); the G0 card's
  return kernel, its stationary limit and its order of limits
  (`../../software/em_charge_attribute/g0_closure_card_v0.md:291–322` and `:387–398`). The G0 drain is ruled out
  (`V3_STEP_PLAN.md:597–601`); the card is a comparison only.
- **The steps that wait on S14a:** S14 (`V3_STEP_PLAN.md:643–646`), and S15's source-map row (`:663–665`). The record
  states which of S14a's four objects are now printed, and the standing of S14, S15 and S20 as the plan gives it.
- **Part 1's carried notes:** the stale status lines and the two notes in
  `directives/_measurements/S14a_1_record_r1_review_disposition.md:35–50`.

## Retained OPEN
- the link between `J` and mass (C3, a gap), and any link to orbital `GM`;
- the core's interior (S22);
- the flow profile (each force's requirement; S21);
- the slab's laws and S12's forms, with the owners the accepted S12 record gives (`steps/S12_order_source.md:292`).
  These include the forms of `Γ_return`, `Γ_drain` and `J_χ`, and the return controllers;
- the material branch, A13 (S1's gate, propagated through S5/S12);
- the readings the user did not choose (above). They are not computed, and no owner is named.

## Intended claim
What the committed law gives at DC, the user's steady-throughput limit, for the projected balances. For a throat at
rest with spherical symmetry: the DC re-ordering, drain and face totals over a shell, the order and material budgets,
`J`'s two channels and the outer flux. How each depends on the outer radius, with exchange live. What the limit
drops. A far-radius limit only where one is printed with its condition. It is not a mass, a drain law, a return form,
a throat solve or a flow.

$ cat research/pde_ledger_v3/directives/S14a_2_decision_list.md
# S14a part 2: steady throughput, the return and the far flux (pre-builder decision list)

**Author:** Claude (orchestrator), 2026-10-21. **Author of the build:** Codex. `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` apply.
Paths are relative to `research/pde_ledger_v3/`. The setup lookups behind each pointer are in
`directives/_measurements/S14a_2_setup_lookups.md`.

**Gate.** This is a pre-builder decision list. It gets one Codex + Grok pass and is folded once (G2). It also names the
objects the build computes, so that content is reviewed until clear (spec row). The builder starts only after that.

**Revision 2.** Revision 2 is the one fold of the G2 pass (dispositions:
`directives/_measurements/S14a_2_decision_list_review_p1_disposition.md`; the pass-1 text is at `d72bb551`). It
defines the limit (Q1, Q2) and names the totals and budgets exactly (Q3).

## Filing and framing
- **The plan's step.** S14a, "The drain bridge" (`V3_STEP_PLAN.md:626`), asks for "the projected order-loss source ·
  the definition of `J` · the profile-dependent `J → Q` map · the controlled `ω → 0` return law" (`:636–637`).
  Until it succeeds, "S14, S15 and S20 are CONDITIONAL, not carried results" (`:639`).
- **Part 1 is accepted** (build `94fddf2f`, comparator `de81b9fc`, record `4a4dfaf5`). It prints the first two
  objects, with flux objects at a finite outer radius (`steps/S14a_1_projection.md:143–150`). It records "No
  steady/no-leakage, constant-density, radius-independent flux or far-radius limit is earned here" (`:140–141`).
  Its OPEN table leaves the return law to "A later part of S14a" and the `J → Q` map with no named later part
  (`:376–377`).
- **User decision, 2026-10-21: this part covers both remaining objects**, the `ω → 0` return law and the `J → Q` map.
- **The slow limit is derived, never posed.** The plan: "If a static limit is ever wanted it must be **derived** as
  the `ω→0` limit of the dynamical law, ⛔ never posed as an independent frozen solve" (`V3_STEP_PLAN.md:599–601`).
  The S12 decision list: "Any slow or static limit is derived from this law, never posed"
  (`directives/S12_decision_list.md:48–49`).
- **The framing carries over** (user, 2026-10-19). This part states what the committed law gives and picks no drain
  law and no return law's form. Each force states the flow it needs, and S21 compares them. Under "Keep S12" (user,
  2026-10-20), the conversion law's forms stay S12's and stay OPEN.

## Supplied equations (committed; unfalsifiable in this build)
The committed law, user decision of 2026-07-24 (`V3_STEP_PLAN.md:589–601`;
`../pde_ledger_v2/notes/stages/ledger_stage006_two_phase_chiB_ontology.md:78–84`):

```
∂_t n + ∇₄·(n u) = 0                                   (total material)
∂_t(χ_B n) + ∇₄·(χ_B n u + J_χ) = n Γ_B ,   Γ_B = Γ_return − Γ_drain ,   [Γ_B] = T⁻¹
```
- **The fields** are part 1's, on the same 4D domain: in-plane `x^i` (three coordinates), bulk direction `w`, and
  time `t`. In this part `Γ_return` and `Γ_drain` are two separate live functions, as the committed law writes them;
  `Γ_B` is their difference. Stage 006's sign: "`Gamma_B < 0` = brane→bulk de-structuring (drain); `Gamma_B > 0` =
  bulk→brane re-ordering (return)" (`:86–87`). No sign is imposed on `Γ_B` or on either part.
- **U1 and its encoding** are as the S12 decision list states them (revision 4, cleared at `b08673a2`): "How U1
  enters", and item 1's "Fields at a face". The shear-holding region is bounded by two live faces
  `w₋(x,t) < w₊(x,t)`, and `χ_B` vanishes outside it. Both are adopted premises, not results of this build.
- **The material factor at a face**, as the accepted S12 record writes it (`steps/S12_order_source.md:79–80`), for
  region `r` at face `s`, using that region's one-sided trace at `w = w_s`:

  ```
  a_{r,s} = u_r^w − ∂_t w_s − u_r^i ∂_i w_s
  ```

  It is supplied and unfalsifiable in this build. The build's face terms are reached by its own integration.
- **Part 1's objects** are the accepted definitions this part takes the limit of: the projected balances (part 1
  item 1) and, in the spherical specialization, the face channel, the core channel, `J`, the order lost, storage and
  the outer flux (part 1 items 2–3; notation `steps/S14a_1_projection.md:80–127`). The SymPy engine may reuse the
  accepted part 1 SymPy construction (`scripts/S14a_1_projection.py`, accepted at `94fddf2f`). The `.wl` imports
  nothing and re-derives.

## User decisions on the limit and the far region (2026-10-21)
The user selected these two options. The wording is the orchestrator's option text.
- **The slow limit: "Steady throughput."** "The throat is at rest, and the drain and re-ordering run at constant
  rates. The pattern stops changing, except that the slab's steady spreading stays a live term. The flow runs
  throughout. No re-ordering form is picked; the forms stay S12's."
- **The far region: "Exchange live."** "Print how the far flux depends on radius, keeping the exchange and storage
  between the throat and the sphere. Take a far-radius limit only where the build shows one exists. Any condition it
  needs is printed, not imposed."

Not chosen: the slowly moving throat, with drive frequency `ω = k·V` (the G0 card's reading,
`../../software/em_charge_attribute/g0_closure_card_v0.md:396`); v2's exchange-free far region as a specialization;
and a split of the far flux into a throat share and a re-ordering share.

**The limit: the DC part (`ω → 0`).** This defines the limit for every item below. The plan calls the frozen
completions it replaces "DC sink/return completions" (`V3_STEP_PLAN.md:633`). No condition is placed on
`n Γ_return`, `n Γ_drain` or any other native field at a point.
- **What it acts on.** The projected objects of part 1 item 1, which are functions of in-plane position and time:
  the time rates of the projected material and order densities, the projected fluxes, the face terms, and the
  projected order source and its parts (below). In the specialization, also the shell objects of items 2 and 3, at
  fixed inner and outer radii. The integration in `w` between the live faces comes first, so no frame in `w` is
  chosen. The in-plane frame is the throat's: the throat is at rest, at the origin of the specialization.
- **The operation.** The DC part of such an object `X` is its zero-frequency part, the long-time average

  ```
  ⟨X⟩ = lim_{T→∞} (1/T) ∫_{t₀}^{t₀+T} X dt
  ```

  at fixed in-plane position, or at fixed radii. Whether it exists is printed as a condition on the live fields,
  never imposed. The DC part of a product or a quotient is the DC part of that whole object.
- **What the limit drops** is `X − ⟨X⟩`, for each object.
- **Steady throughput, as the user chose it.** The DC parts do not change in time, so the drain and re-ordering run
  at constant rates. The DC part of each projected density's time rate is the slab's steady spreading, and it stays
  live. No DC part is set to zero, and the slab may spread by thickening, by growing in area, or both.
- **Order of limits.** The average is taken at fixed position and fixed radii. Any far-radius limit (item 3) is
  taken after it, on the DC objects.

**The parts of the projected order source.** Part 1's projected order source is `Sχ = ∫n₂Γ_B,2 dw + C₋ + C₊`
(`steps/S14a_1_projection.md:89`), where `C₋` and `C₊` are its parts concentrated on the faces (`:87`). With
`Γ_B = Γ_return − Γ_drain`:
- the **return part** is `∫n₂Γ_return,2 dw` and the **drain part** is `∫n₂Γ_drain,2 dw`, each over the regular part
  inside the region only;
- the **face part** is `C₋ + C₊`, net. It is not split into return and drain: no such split is supplied.

Each concentrated part is counted once, in the face part.

## What the build computes
Both engines construct these independently, with the `.wl` blind.

1. **The DC part of the projected balances.** Apply the operation above to both time-dependent projected balances
   of part 1 item 1.
   - Print the DC part of each balance. The DC parts of the return, drain and face parts of the projected order
     source are printed separately.
   - **What the limit drops.** For each balance, print the time-dependent object, its DC part and what the limit
     drops, so nothing is dropped unseen.
   - Every field stays live.
2. **The return at DC.** In part 1's labelled specialization (spherical symmetry about the throat, the shell between
   an inner sphere around the excluded core and an outer sphere), print the DC parts of, separately:
   - **the re-ordering total, the drain total and the face total:** the return, drain and face parts integrated over
     the shell, `∫shell` as part 1 writes it (`steps/S14a_1_projection.md:112`);
   - **the order lost** over the shell, as part 1 defines it (`Lχ = −∫shell Sχ`, `:125`);
   - **the order budget over the shell:** part 1's projected order balance (`:91`) integrated over the shell. Its
     terms are the storage of projected order, `∂t∫shell Σχ`; the in-plane order flux `4πr²H_r` (`H` as at `:84`),
     outward through the outer sphere and inward through the inner sphere, as part 1 orients its outer flux and core
     channel (`:126`, `:123`); the face terms as that balance carries them, after the full concentrated part
     (`:88`); and `∫shell Sχ`, as the three totals;
   - **the material budget over the shell:** part 1's face channel, core channel, `J`, storage and outer flux, and
     the balance relating them.

   For each budget, print the time-dependent object, its DC part and what the limit drops.
3. **The far flux with exchange live** (user decision above). In the same specialization, print as functions of the
   outer radius:
   - the DC part of every object of item 2;
   - the DC part of the outer material flux, and, where the projected material density at the outer radius is
     nonzero over the average, the DC part of the volume flux of the projected velocity through the outer sphere.
     That condition is carried wherever the velocity or its volume flux is printed;
   - the dependence of each on the outer radius, with the exchange and storage between the inner and outer spheres
     kept.

   A far-radius limit is printed only where the build shows it exists, after the average. The condition on the live
   fields that it needs is printed as an object, never imposed.
4. **Dimensional homogeneity** of every new object in items 1–3.

A name may name an object, never its value or a comparison outcome. The script prints objects and never states
conclusions. No acceptance criterion references an expected value. Spherical symmetry is labelled as a
specialization wherever it is printed; it is not a limit earned from item 1. The DC part is labelled as the user's
steady-throughput limit wherever it is printed.

## Model point
- **Regime:** the DC part (`ω → 0`), the user's steady throughput (2026-10-21), reached from the time-dependent
  balances by the operation above. The time-dependent objects and what the limit drops are printed beside it.
- **Freezes:** none intended. The operation acts on whole projected objects and sets no DC part to zero. `J_χ`
  stays live, the faces keep their motion, and no exchange sign is imposed: U2 leaves the sign free. U3 and U4 enter no
  computed object, as in S12 (`steps/S12_order_source.md:154–155`). The user's premise that brane material leaves only
  in throats (2026-10-06) is not imposed.
- **Transfer:** wherever the committed law holds and the shear-holding region has two faces over the in-plane
  coordinates. The puncture's core is excluded and stays with S22.

  - `../../software/stage1_solver/reports/pathA_21_emergent_G_mass_bridge.md:19`, `:22` and `:62–66`: the rows for

```
$ sed -n '78,84p;86,87p' /var/projects/toy_physics/research/pde_ledger_v2/notes/stages/ledger_stage006_two_phase_chiB_ontology.md | cut -c1-200
Balance laws:

```
∂_t n + div_4(n u) = 0                                    (total, exact)
∂_t(chi_B n) + div_4(chi_B n u + J_chi) = n Gamma_B ,      [Gamma_B] = T^-1
Gamma_B = Gamma_return - Gamma_drain
```
`Gamma_B < 0` = brane→bulk de-structuring (drain); `Gamma_B > 0` = bulk→brane
re-ordering (return). Total material is conserved; brane-ORDER is not.
````````

## M03 — Both review dispositions, lookup existence and filed assembly audit

````````text
$ python3 research/pde_ledger_v3/scripts/S14a_2_record_measurements.py --section M03
$ cat research/pde_ledger_v3/directives/_measurements/S14a_2_decision_list_review_p1_disposition.md
# S14a part 2 decision list, review pass 1: dispositions (orchestrator)

The mechanical lookups behind each disposition are in `S14a_2_decision_list_review_p1_lookups.md`, beside this file
(generator `_scratch/s14a/gen/s14a2_dl_p1_lookups.sh`). "K-n" cites a section of that file. Every computed result
quoted here is a leg's saved stdout.

## The pass

- **Artifact.** `directives/S14a_2_decision_list.md`, orchestrator-written, sha256 `08da185f…`, with
  `S14a_2_setup_lookups.md`. Both are preserved unchanged in the commit that adds this file. Both matched the
  baseline after the pass, and the working tree was unchanged apart from the lookups file itself (K1).
- **Role.** A pre-builder decision list (G2: one Codex + Grok pass, fold once). It also names the objects the build
  computes, so that content is reviewed until clear (spec row).
- **Prompt and legs.** `_scratch/s14a2/dl_review_prompt_p1.md`, sha256 `2f80f58b…` (K1). Both legs received it
  identically, in parallel. Codex (xhigh, workspace-write in its scratch) wrote
  `/var/projects/_review_scratch/s14a2-dl-p1-codex/REVIEW.md`, and Grok (`grok-4.7`, effort high) wrote
  `/var/projects/_review_scratch/s14a2-dl-p1-grok/REVIEW.md`. Both exited 0 (K1).
- **Verdicts.** Codex: FINDINGS, 3. Grok: FINDINGS, 2 (K2). Each finding below carries its leg's script and filed
  stdout.
- **What Grok checked and found sound:** the displayed balances against stage 006 and the plan, the face factor,
  item 2's material budget as part 1's objects, the readings not chosen kept out of the computed items, the OPEN
  owners, and the labelled specialization.

## Findings

**Q1. "Constant rates" is posed pointwise, on the source densities.** (Codex 1, Grok 1.) **CONFIRMED.**
- **The list.** "The drain and re-ordering source densities, `n Γ_drain` and `n Γ_return`, are independent of
  time. Each stays a live function of position" (l.71–72, K3).
- **The decision it makes operational.** "The throat is at rest, and the drain and re-ordering run at constant
  rates. The pattern stops changing, except that the slab's steady spreading stays a live term" (l.59–61, K3). It
  puts no condition on `n Γ` at each point.
- **The evidence.**
  - Codex: a spreading slab whose return and drain totals are constant (time derivatives `(0, 0)`) while the
    return's first `w` moment grows, `5/16` per unit time in units of `4π`. The support moves, so the source
    densities at a point do change. Conversely, source densities that are independent of time at each point
    (`(1, 0)`) give totals that grow at `7/12` (K4).
  - Grok: homologous spreading with a rate constant along the spreading coordinate satisfies both balances, yet
    `∂t(n Γ_return) = -2*n0*s/h0` at a sample point. The `ω → 0` limit of its modulated family has the same value,
    so the operational premise excludes it. Grok also shows that `∂t Γ = 0` and `∂t(n Γ) = 0` are different
    conditions (K4).
- **Effect.** The build would take a limit other than the one the decision names, adding a pointwise condition
  and still not making the totals constant.
- **What must be true.** "Constant rates" is stated for the rate objects the decision names, with their integration
  domain and measure, and with the frame in which they are constant. No condition is added that the decision does
  not contain.

**Q2. The limit is not defined, so "what the limit drops" is not one object.** (Codex 2, Grok 1.) **CONFIRMED.**
- **The list.** "What steady throughput requires of `n`, `u`, `χ_B`, `J_χ` and the faces is derived by the build
  from the time-dependent balances" (l.76–77). Item 1: "take the limit as defined above", and print "the
  time-dependent object, the limit object and their difference" (l.82–88, K3).
- **The source.** The plan: a static limit "must be **derived** as the `ω→0` limit of the dynamical law, ⛔ never
  posed as an independent frozen solve" (l.599–601, K7).
- **The evidence.**
  - Codex: a solution with source densities independent of time and constant shell totals, whose storage
    `7*cos(t)/12` and core channel `cos(t)/12 + 1` oscillate. Every balance residual is `0` (K5). The balances
    admit it, and the list's definition does not exclude it.
  - Codex: one slow family gives storage `0` at fixed `t` as `ε → 0`, but `7*cos(τ)/12` (divided by `ε`) at
    `t = τ/ε` (K5). The limit depends on the family and its time scaling, and the list gives neither.
  - Grok: within the operational premise, family A has storage `0` and family S has storage `n0*s`. Family E keeps
    a secular change in `χ`, with both source-density derivatives `0` (K5). There is no single persisting part.
- **Effect.** Items 2 and 3 are taken in item 1's limit, so they inherit the gap. The plan's controlled `ω → 0`
  obligation could not be claimed.
- **What must be true.** The list defines the limit itself: the operation that takes any time-dependent solution to
  its limit, and its order relative to the projection and to any far-radius limit. What the limit drops is then one
  object for each printed object. The limit is reached from the time-dependent law, never posed, and leaves S12's
  forms open.

**Q3. The shell totals and the order budget do not name one assembly.** (Codex 3, Grok 2.) **CONFIRMED.**
- **The list.** "the re-ordering total over the shell, from `Γ_return`, and the drain total, from `Γ_drain`"; the
  order budget's "order crossing the faces" and "in-plane order flux through each sphere" (l.93–101, K3).
- **The sources.**
  - Part 1 prints two order-leaving objects at each face, before and after the full concentrated part, and its
    projected order balance uses the second. Its projected order source includes the concentrated parts `C₋`, `C₊`
    (record l.88–91, K6).
  - The S12 decision list: the parts of `n Γ_B` concentrated on a face are part of the source (l.97–98, K6).
  - Part 1's orientations: the outer flux is outward and the core channel inward (record l.126 and l.123, K6).
    Its order lost carries a minus sign and includes the face parts (part 1 decision list l.85–86, K6).
- **The evidence.**
  - Codex: for the same fields, totals over the regular part are `(0, 0)` and totals over the full source are
    `(2, 1)`, in units of `4π`. Adding the separately printed face source to full totals leaves `-1` (K6).
  - Grok: the before-face terms with the full source leave `-2` on a sample. Taking both sphere fluxes outward leaves
    `2*a**2*H_r(a)`, `32` on a sample. The two sign choices for the totals differ by
    `2*int_n_Gamma_drain - 2*int_n_Gamma_return` (K6).
- **What must be true.** Each total names its integrand and measure, and whether face-concentrated parts are in it.
  Each concentrated part is counted exactly once. The face terms in the order budget are named as one of part 1's
  two objects. Each sphere's flux carries its orientation. The signs follow from `Γ_B = Γ_return − Γ_drain`.

## Agreement between the legs

Both legs found Q1 and Q2; Codex reported them as two findings, Grok as one. Both found Q3, each with its own
witness. Neither leg reported a finding the other contradicts.

## Adjudication

Q1 to Q3 each change what is computed. All three are folded once into the list (G2). The fold changes the named
objects, so the folded content is reviewed until clear: a Codex + Grok pass on the folded list runs before any
builder.

**For the orchestrator's own record.** Q1 is a form the orchestrator dictated. "Made operational here" turned the
decision's "constant rates" into a pointwise condition the decision does not contain.

# S14a part 2 decision list, review pass 2: dispositions (orchestrator)

The mechanical lookups behind each disposition are in `S14a_2_decision_list_review_p2_lookups.md`, beside this file
(generator `_scratch/s14a/gen/s14a2_dl_p2_lookups.sh`). "M-n" cites a section of that file. Every computed result
quoted here is a leg's saved stdout.

## The pass

- **Artifact.** `directives/S14a_2_decision_list.md`, revision 2, committed at `945a1cd2` (sha256 `c7d8fd26…`), with
  `S14a_2_setup_lookups.md`. Both matched the baseline after the pass, and the working tree was unchanged apart from
  the lookups file itself (M1).
- **Role.** The physics content of a pre-builder decision list, reviewed until clear (spec row). The G2 pass and its
  one fold are complete (`S14a_2_decision_list_review_p1_disposition.md`; fold `945a1cd2`).
- **Prompt and legs.** `_scratch/s14a2/dl_review_prompt_p2.md`, sha256 `4df59427…` (M1). Item 0 checks the fold;
  items 1–7 review the list again. Both legs received it identically, in parallel: Codex (xhigh, workspace-write in
  its scratch), `/var/projects/_review_scratch/s14a2-dl-p2-codex/REVIEW.md`; Grok (`grok-4.7`, effort high),
  `/var/projects/_review_scratch/s14a2-dl-p2-grok/REVIEW.md`. Both exited 0 (M1).
- **Verdicts.** Codex: FINDINGS, 2. Grok: FINDINGS, 1 (M2).

## The fold, checked

- **Q3 is repaired.** Both legs. Codex's assembly audit closes the order budget with the regular return and drain
  totals, the net face part counted once, the face terms after the full concentrated part and the inner flux
  inward: residual `0` (M7). Grok's signed and orientation identities agree (M2).
- **Q1's pointwise condition is gone.** Both legs. Codex finds that its replacement identifies constant averages
  with the selected steady solution; that is R1 below.
- **Q2's operation is defined.** Grok finds it definite on the objects it acts on. Codex finds that its
  identification with the dynamical `ω → 0` limit, and its order relative to the far-radius limit, are outstanding;
  those are R1 and R2.

## Findings

**R1. The long-time average is not the `ω → 0` limit of the dynamical law.** (Codex 1.) **CONFIRMED.**
- **The list.** "The DC part of such an object `X` is its zero-frequency part, the long-time average", labelled
  "`ω → 0`" and "the user's steady-throughput limit" (l.74–93, M3).
- **The source.** The plan: the limit must be "**derived** as the `ω→0` limit of the dynamical law" (l.599–601,
  M8).
- **The evidence.**
  - Codex's family A solves both committed balances for every `ω`, with constant shell return and drain totals and a
    slab growing at constant speed. Its `J/(4π)` is `17/12 - cos(omega*t)`. The long-time average gives `17/12`. The
    family's `ω → 0` limit at fixed time gives `5/12`. The part the average drops contributes `-1` in that limit
    (M4).
  - Codex's family B, with a strictly positive density: the average of the whole volume-flux quotient is
    `-4*sqrt(15)/15`, and its `ω → 0` value is `-4/5`, in units of `4π` (M4).
- **Effect.** Both values belong to steady states of the committed balances. The list labels one of them as "the
  `ω → 0` limit" without deriving it as such.

**R2. Averaging at fixed radii before the far-radius limit can remove spreading and exchange that persist.**
(Codex 2.) **CONFIRMED.**
- **The list.** "The average is taken at fixed position and fixed radii. Any far-radius limit (item 3) is taken
  after it, on the DC objects" (l.94–95). "The DC part of each projected density's time rate is the slab's steady
  spreading, and it stays live" (l.92–93, M3).
- **The decision.** "Exchange live": keep "the exchange and storage between the throat and the sphere" (l.66–68).
- **The evidence.** Codex's spreading-front family solves both balances and the full shell budgets. Its global
  storage, net face source and `J` are constant in time: `1/6`, `7/12` and `11/6` in units of `4π`. Averaged at a
  fixed radius first, every finite shell gives storage `0`, face channel `0` and face source `0`, and the far limit
  keeps those zeros. Taking the far limit first gives storage `1/6` and face channel `-7/6`. The outer material
  flux is `-3` in one order and `-2` in the other (M5).
- **Effect.** The fixed order can remove, at every radius, the spreading and exchange that the decision keeps live.
  Existence of the averages and the far limits does not detect this.

**R3. "Nonzero over the average" is not one domain for the volume flux.** (Grok 1.) **CONFIRMED.**
- **The list.** The volume flux is printed "where the projected material density at the outer radius is nonzero
  over the average" (l.133–135, M3). Part 1's condition was `Σ(R,t) ≠ 0` (part 1 decision list l.93–98, M8).
- **The evidence.** Grok's solution has `Σ = c t`, nonzero for every `t > 0`, with no finite average. The average of
  the whole volume-flux quotient exists and equals `4*pi*beta/c`. Reading the gate as "the average of `Σ` is finite
  and nonzero" prints nothing; reading it as "`Σ ≠ 0` over the window and the quotient's average exists" prints
  `4*pi*beta/c`. On this solution the separate averages of the outer flux and the core channel diverge, while
  their balance with storage averages to `0` (M6).
- **Effect.** Item 3 prints a different object under each reading, and one of them omits the throughput on a
  steadily spreading solution.

## Agreement between the legs

Both legs found Q3 repaired and Q1's pointwise condition removed. On Q2, Grok found the operation definite on its
objects and raised only R3; Codex found R1 and R2. These do not conflict: Grok's finding concerns the domain of one
printed object, while Codex's concern the identification and order of the limit itself. Lookup sections M4 and M5 bear
out Codex's two witnesses as filed.

## Adjudication: the premise goes to the user

R1–R3 each change what is computed or what may be claimed. They are not folded here.

**The orchestrator's reading of passes 1 and 2 together.** Pass 1's Q2 and this pass's R1 and R2 have one root.
The committed law gives two balances for more unknown fields, with every form open, so it does not fix how a
solution evolves. A slow limit then needs a family of solutions and an order of limits, and the committed law
selects neither. Each definition written so far has picked one: a pointwise condition in revision 1, the long-time
average at fixed radii in revision 2. Each time the legs found solutions of the committed balances where another
choice gives another value (pass 1 K5; this pass M4, M5). This is a reading of the filed witnesses, not a computed
result of a build.

Under the standing rules on long loops and on many review rounds, the next step is a question to the user, not a
third revision: whether to narrow the claim, record the obligation as needing the slab's dynamics, or stop.
````````

````````text
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Small admissible shell witness, not a construction of the proposed build.

All rates below are divided by the common angular factor 4*pi. Distribution
moments use the stipulated full weight at each closed-slab endpoint.
"""
import resource
resource.setrlimit(resource.RLIMIT_AS, (1024**3, 1024**3))
import sympy as s

r, t, w = s.symbols('r t w', real=True)
lo, hi = s.Integer(0), 1 + t/4
n, ur, uw = r**-2, s.Integer(-2), s.Integer(-1)
radial_div = lambda f: s.diff(r*r*f, r)/(r*r)
shell = lambda f: s.integrate(r*r*f, (r, 1, 2))
material = s.diff(n,t) + radial_div(n*ur) + s.diff(n*uw,w)
a_lo, a_hi = uw-s.diff(lo,t), uw-s.diff(hi,t)
# Directly differentiate the ordered current. Exterior J_chi is zero here.
chi = s.Heaviside(w-lo)-s.Heaviside(w-hi)
order_density, order_radial, order_w = n*chi, n*chi*ur, n*chi*uw
order_source = s.expand(s.diff(order_density,t)+radial_div(order_radial)+s.diff(order_w,w))
low_atom = s.simplify(order_source.coeff(s.DiracDelta(w)))
high_atom = s.simplify(order_source.coeff(s.DiracDelta(w-hi)))
ret, drain = high_atom, -low_atom
ret_total, drain_total = shell(ret), shell(drain)
ret_first_w_moment = shell(ret*hi)
sigma, flux = n*(hi-lo), n*ur*(hi-lo)
storage = s.diff(shell(sigma),t)
face = shell(-n*a_lo+n*a_hi)
core = -(r*r*flux).subs(r,1)
outer = (r*r*flux).subs(r,2)

print('domain: 1 <= r <= 2; t >= 0; 0 <= w <= 1+t/4; core r<1 excluded')
print('fields: n=1/r^2 in all regions, u_r=-2, u_w=-1, interior chi=1, J_chi=0')
print('material regular residual =', material)
print('material face jump residuals =', (s.simplify(n*a_lo-n*a_lo),s.simplify(n*a_hi-n*a_hi)))
print('order-source lower/upper delta coefficients =', (low_atom,high_atom))
print('return total/(4*pi) =', ret_total)
print('drain total/(4*pi) =', drain_total)
print('time derivatives of those totals =', (s.diff(ret_total,t),s.diff(drain_total,t)))
print('return first w moment/(4*pi) =', ret_first_w_moment)
print('time derivative of return first w moment/(4*pi) =', s.diff(ret_first_w_moment,t))
print('projected material storage/(4*pi) =', storage)
print('material shell budget residual/(4*pi) =', s.simplify(storage+face+core+outer))
print('order shell budget residual/(4*pi) =', s.simplify(storage+core+outer-ret_total+drain_total))

# Converse witness: stationary ordinary source densities over a moving slab
# need not yield constant integrated rates, even without any source atoms.
n2, ur2 = s.Integer(1), -2/r**2
f2 = w*(hi-w)/hi**2
primitive2 = s.integrate(f2,w)
j2w = -s.diff(primitive2,t)
j2top = s.simplify(j2w.subs(w,hi))
regular_material2 = s.diff(n2,t)+radial_div(n2*ur2)
regular_order2 = s.simplify(s.diff(f2,t)+radial_div(f2*ur2)+s.diff(j2w,w))
source_density2 = s.Integer(1)  # Both independent inputs n*Gamma_return and n*Gamma_drain.
each_total2 = shell(source_density2*(hi-lo))
atom2_lower = s.simplify(f2.subs(w,lo)*(-s.diff(lo,t))+j2w.subs(w,lo))
atom2_upper = s.simplify(-f2.subs(w,hi)*(-s.diff(hi,t))+j2top-j2w.subs(w,hi))
print('converse witness: n=1, u_r=-2/r^2, u_w=0, chi_inside=w*(h-w)/h^2, h=1+t/4')
print('converse J_chi^w inside =',s.factor(j2w))
print('converse J_chi^w below/above =',(0,j2top))
print('converse material/order regular residuals =',(s.simplify(regular_material2),regular_order2))
print('converse order face coefficients =',(atom2_lower,atom2_upper))
print('converse each source density/time derivative =',(source_density2,s.diff(source_density2,t)))
print('converse each return/drain total/(4*pi) =',each_total2)
print('converse time derivative of each total/(4*pi) =',s.diff(each_total2,t))

$ cat research/pde_ledger_v3/directives/_measurements/S14a_2_review_witnesses/p1-codex_check_steady_source_motion.stdout
domain: 1 <= r <= 2; t >= 0; 0 <= w <= 1+t/4; core r<1 excluded
fields: n=1/r^2 in all regions, u_r=-2, u_w=-1, interior chi=1, J_chi=0
material regular residual = 0
material face jump residuals = (0, 0)
order-source lower/upper delta coefficients = (-1/r**2, 5/(4*r**2))
return total/(4*pi) = 5/4
drain total/(4*pi) = 1
time derivatives of those totals = (0, 0)
return first w moment/(4*pi) = 5*t/16 + 5/4
time derivative of return first w moment/(4*pi) = 5/16
projected material storage/(4*pi) = 1/4
material shell budget residual/(4*pi) = 0
order shell budget residual/(4*pi) = 0
converse witness: n=1, u_r=-2/r^2, u_w=0, chi_inside=w*(h-w)/h^2, h=1+t/4

converse J_chi^w below/above = (0, -1/24)
converse material/order regular residuals = (0, 0)
converse order face coefficients = (0, 0)
converse each source density/time derivative = (1, 0)
converse each return/drain total/(4*pi) = 7*t/12 + 7/3
converse time derivative of each total/(4*pi) = 7/12
````````

## M05 — Pass 1 Codex: constant sources do not select a limit

````````text
$ python3 research/pde_ledger_v3/scripts/S14a_2_record_measurements.py --section M05
$ cat research/pde_ledger_v3/directives/_measurements/S14a_2_review_witnesses/p1-codex_check_limit_not_selected.py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Exact spherical witness to the information absent from constant sources.

This only checks a single family against the supplied laws. It neither solves
for source forms nor implements the requested S14a build.
"""
import resource
resource.setrlimit(resource.RLIMIT_AS, (1024**3, 1024**3))
import sympy as s

r,t,w,eps,tau = s.symbols('r t w epsilon tau', real=True)
n = 1+s.sin(t)/4
ur = (-r*s.cos(t)/12-r**-2)/n
gamma_ret = gamma_drain = 1/n
radial_div = lambda f: s.diff(r*r*f,r)/(r*r)
shell = lambda f: s.integrate(r*r*f,(r,1,2))
material = s.simplify(s.diff(n,t)+radial_div(n*ur))
order = s.simplify(s.diff(n/2,t)+radial_div(n*ur/2)-n*(gamma_ret-gamma_drain))
chi=s.Rational(1,2)*(s.Heaviside(w)-s.Heaviside(w-1))
global_order=s.expand(s.diff(n*chi,t)+radial_div(n*ur*chi))
face_coefficients=tuple(s.simplify(global_order.coeff(s.DiracDelta(w-face))) for face in (0,1))
storage = s.simplify(s.diff(shell(n),t))
core = s.simplify(-(r*r*n*ur).subs(r,1))
outer = s.simplify((r*r*n*ur).subs(r,2))

print('domain: 1 <= r <= 2; fixed faces w=0,1; all real t; core r<1 excluded')
print('fields: n=1+sin(t)/4; u_r=(-r*cos(t)/12-1/r^2)/n; u_w=0')
print('interior chi=1/2; exterior chi=0; J_chi=0; Gamma_return=Gamma_drain=1/n')
print('density range =',(1-abs(n.coeff(s.sin(t))),1+abs(n.coeff(s.sin(t)))))
print('material regular residual =',material)
print('order regular residual =',order)
print('order face coefficients =',face_coefficients)
print('material face jump coefficients =',tuple(s.simplify(n*0-n*0) for face in (0,1)))
print('source densities =',(s.simplify(n*gamma_ret),s.simplify(n*gamma_drain)))
print('source-density time derivatives =',(s.diff(s.simplify(n*gamma_ret),t),s.diff(s.simplify(n*gamma_drain),t)))
print('return/drain shell totals/(4*pi) =',(shell(n*gamma_ret),shell(n*gamma_drain)))
print('projected density time derivative =',s.diff(n,t))
print('material storage/(4*pi) =',storage)
print('core channel J/(4*pi) =',core)
print('material shell budget residual/(4*pi) =',s.simplify(storage+core+outer))
print('density at t=0, pi/2 =',(n.subs(t,0),n.subs(t,s.pi/2)))

# One possible slow family: the source densities remain exactly the same.
n_eps = 1+s.sin(eps*t)/4
ur_eps = (-eps*r*s.cos(eps*t)/12-r**-2)/n_eps
K_eps = s.simplify(s.diff(shell(n_eps),t))
print('possible slow-family material residual =',s.simplify(s.diff(n_eps,t)+radial_div(n_eps*ur_eps)))
print('fixed-t epsilon->0 density/storage =',(s.limit(n_eps,eps,0),s.limit(K_eps,eps,0)))
print('at t=tau/epsilon density/storage divided by epsilon =',(n_eps.subs(t,tau/eps),s.simplify((K_eps/eps).subs(t,tau/eps))))

$ cat research/pde_ledger_v3/directives/_measurements/S14a_2_review_witnesses/p1-codex_check_limit_not_selected.stdout
domain: 1 <= r <= 2; fixed faces w=0,1; all real t; core r<1 excluded
fields: n=1+sin(t)/4; u_r=(-r*cos(t)/12-1/r^2)/n; u_w=0
interior chi=1/2; exterior chi=0; J_chi=0; Gamma_return=Gamma_drain=1/n
density range = (3/4, 5/4)
material regular residual = 0
order regular residual = 0
order face coefficients = (0, 0)
material face jump coefficients = (0, 0)
source densities = (1, 1)
source-density time derivatives = (0, 0)
return/drain shell totals/(4*pi) = (7/3, 7/3)

material storage/(4*pi) = 7*cos(t)/12
core channel J/(4*pi) = cos(t)/12 + 1

density at t=0, pi/2 = (1, 5/4)
possible slow-family material residual = 0
fixed-t epsilon->0 density/storage = (1, 0)
at t=tau/epsilon density/storage divided by epsilon = (sin(tau)/4 + 1, 7*cos(tau)/12)
````````

## M06 — Pass 1 Codex: regular versus full face-source totals

````````text
$ python3 research/pde_ledger_v3/scripts/S14a_2_record_measurements.py --section M06
$ cat research/pde_ledger_v3/directives/_measurements/S14a_2_review_witnesses/p1-codex_check_face_total_scope.py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""A fixed admissible field configuration distinguishes shell total meanings.

Full face weights and rates divided by 4*pi, as in the other diagnostics.
"""
import resource
resource.setrlimit(resource.RLIMIT_AS, (1024**3, 1024**3))
import sympy as s

r,w,t=s.symbols('r w t', real=True)
n=r**-2
ur,uw=s.Integer(-2),s.Integer(-1)
chi=s.Heaviside(w)-s.Heaviside(w-1)
# Extra order flux is zero below/inside, 1/r^2 above: live regional fields,
# with a permitted jump and no concentrated input part.
jw=s.Heaviside(w-1)/r**2
divr=lambda f:s.diff(r*r*f,r)/(r*r)
source=s.expand(s.diff(n*chi,t)+divr(n*chi*ur)+s.diff(n*chi*uw+jw,w))
c_lo=s.simplify(source.coeff(s.DiracDelta(w)))
c_hi=s.simplify(source.coeff(s.DiracDelta(w-1)))
shell=lambda f:s.integrate(r*r*f,(r,1,2))
face_source=shell(c_lo+c_hi)
# An explicit independent source split for this witness, rather than a rule
# assigning positive/negative parts in the general problem.
return_measure=2*s.DiracDelta(w-1)/r**2
drain_measure=s.DiracDelta(w)/r**2
full_return=shell(return_measure.coeff(s.DiracDelta(w-1)))
full_drain=shell(drain_measure.coeff(s.DiracDelta(w)))
# Exterior order flux supplies the closed-projection face term.
face_order_flux=shell(n)
regular_source=s.simplify(source-c_lo*s.DiracDelta(w)-c_hi*s.DiracDelta(w-1))
regular_return=regular_drain=s.Integer(0)

print('domain: 1 <= r <= 2; fixed faces w=0,1; core r<1 excluded')
print('fields: n=1/r^2, u_r=-2, u_w=-1 in all regions; interior chi=1')
print('J_chi^w: below=0, inside=0, above=1/r^2; all other J_chi components=0')
print('material regular residual =',s.simplify(s.diff(n,t)+divr(n*ur)+s.diff(n*uw,w)))
print('material face jump residuals =',tuple(s.simplify(n*uw-n*uw) for face in (0,1)))
print('regular order source =',regular_source)
print('computed lower/upper face source coefficients =',(c_lo,c_hi))
print('chosen full source split minus computed order source =',s.simplify(return_measure-drain_measure-source))
print('regular-only return/drain totals/(4*pi) =',(shell(regular_return),shell(regular_drain)))
print('full-source return/drain totals/(4*pi) =',(full_return,full_drain))
print('separately printed face-source total/(4*pi) =',face_source)
print('net in-plane order flux through both spheres/(4*pi) =',s.simplify((-r*r*n*ur).subs(r,1)+(r*r*n*ur).subs(r,2)))
print('closed-projection order flux through faces/(4*pi) =',face_order_flux)
print('order budget residual with regular totals plus face source =',s.simplify(face_order_flux-face_source))
print('order budget residual with full totals, faces counted within them =',s.simplify(face_order_flux-full_return+full_drain))
print('order budget residual if full totals plus face source are added =',s.simplify(face_order_flux-full_return+full_drain-face_source))

$ cat research/pde_ledger_v3/directives/_measurements/S14a_2_review_witnesses/p1-codex_check_face_total_scope.stdout
domain: 1 <= r <= 2; fixed faces w=0,1; core r<1 excluded
fields: n=1/r^2, u_r=-2, u_w=-1 in all regions; interior chi=1
J_chi^w: below=0, inside=0, above=1/r^2; all other J_chi components=0

material face jump residuals = (0, 0)
regular order source = 0
computed lower/upper face source coefficients = (-1/r**2, 2/r**2)
chosen full source split minus computed order source = 0

full-source return/drain totals/(4*pi) = (2, 1)
separately printed face-source total/(4*pi) = 1
net in-plane order flux through both spheres/(4*pi) = 0
````````
